<page-num>68</page-num>
<header>ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.</header>

Indeed, we have discovered that Mani <note>original: "Manetem"; refers to the 3rd-century founder of Manichaeism, a religion that viewed the world as a struggle between light and dark.</note> established this in the same way, based on Marcellus's outline of his doctrines, which is found in the works of Epiphanius <note>Epiphanius of Salamis (c. 310–403), a bishop known for his "Panarion," a massive refutation of various heresies.</note> and was repeated by the illustrious Zacagni in his *Collection of Ancient Monuments*. In those pages, on page 10, you may read these words: <note>original Greek: "τὸν δὲ Θεὸν μὴ ἔχειν μέρος μετ' αὐτοῦ τοῦ κόσμου, μηδὲ χαίρειν ἐπ' αὐτῷ, διὰ τὸ ἐξ ἀρχῆς σεσυλῆσθαι αὐτὸν ὑπὸ τῶν ἀρχόντων, καὶ γενέσθαι αὐτῷ θλίψιν."</note> "God has no part with this world, nor does he rejoice in it, because from the beginning he was despoiled by the rulers <note>In Gnostic and Manichaean thought, 'Rulers' (Archons) were malevolent spiritual beings who created the physical world.</note>, and there arose for him distress." I pass over the rest, those false citizens of the Church who pronounced the same opinion, whom I have mentioned by design in my work *Manichaeism before the Manichaeans*. This same opinion, which the Bogomils had absorbed, they attempted to confirm with foolish interpretations of passages from Scripture. To this end, they distort Matthew 4:8, where they interpret the "mountain" as the second heaven; they think that Satan would not have approached it unless he remembered it had been built by him. The words of Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the primary source for Bogomil beliefs.</note> from Section XXXIII are as follows: <note>original Greek: "Ὄρος ὑψηλὸν λέγουσι τὸν δεύτερον οὐρανὸν, εἰς ὃν τὸν χριστὸν ἀναβιβασθῆναι παρὰ τοῦ διαβόλου καὶ ἰδεῖν πάσας τὰς βασιλείας τοῦ κόσμου. Καὶ φασιν, ὅτι οὐκ ἂν ὁ διάβολος ἀνῆλθεν εἰς αὐτὸν, εἰ μὴ ποίημα τοῦτον ἴδιον ἐγίνωσκεν, οὐδ' ἂν ἑαυτῷ παραδεδόσθαι πάσας τὰς βασιλείας εἶπεν, εἰ μὴ καὶ τὴν τούτων συνεχωρήθη κυριαρχίαν, ὡς παρ' αὐτοῦ γεγενημένην, καθὼς προδεδήλωται."</note> "They say the high mountain is the second heaven, into which Christ was led up by the devil to see all the kingdoms of the world. And they say that the devil would not have gone up into it if he did not know it as his own creation, nor would he have said that all the kingdoms were delivered to him, unless he had been granted sovereignty over them as having been made by him, as was declared before." In a similar way, they explain Matthew 5:35, thinking that Satan is designated by the name of the 'Great King': <note>original Greek: "μέγαν βασιλέα... λέγοντες νῦν τὸν διάβολον, ὡς κοσμοκράτορα."</note> "now calling the devil the great king," says Euthymius in Section XL, "as the ruler of this world <term>cosmocrator|A Greek term meaning 'world-ruler,' often used in the New Testament to describe spiritual powers of evil.</term>." He continues: <note>original Greek: "ἵλεως ἡμῖν... εἴης κύριε, τὰς ἐκείνων βλασφημίας διηγουμένοις."</note> "May you be merciful to us, O Lord, as we relate their blasphemies."

### XIV. 
Let us now hear Euthymius’s refutation, from which greater light will be shed upon the opinion of the Bogomils themselves. <note>original Greek: "ταῦτα μὲν... ἐκείνων τὰ μυθικά. ἀλλὰ πῶς οὐκ ἠδύνατο τοῖς ὕδασιν ἐφιζάνειν; μᾶλλον δὲ ἐπειδὴ τὸ ἐφιζάνειν σώματός ἐστι, πῶς οὐκ ἠδύνατο μετὰ τῶν συναποστατικῶν δυνάμεων ἔχειν τὸ ὕδωρ ἐνδιαιτητήριον; καὶ γὰρ καὶ νῦν ὕδασιν ἐνδιαιτῶνται, καὶ πηγαῖς, καὶ πο-"</note> "These things," he says, "are their fables. But how could he [Satan] not rest upon the waters? Or rather, since 'resting' is an attribute of a body, how could he not have had the water as a dwelling place along with his fellow-apostate powers? For even now they dwell in waters, and springs, and ri—"

The author concludes with the beginning of Euthymius Zigabenus's theological refutation of these claims.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the refutation of the Bogomil creation myth, specifically addressing the nature of their fallen figure Satanael and how they cherry-pick from the Book of Genesis despite officially rejecting the Old Testament.</meta>

<page-num>69</page-num>
<header>ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.</header>
<sig>K 3</sig>

...in rivers, and lakes, and seas; and they remain underground as immaterial spirits, much more immaterial than the spirits of the winds. But if he <note>Referring to Satanael, whom the Bogomils believed was the elder brother of Christ and the creator of the physical world.</note> were also by nature a son of God, he would be equal in power to the Father by nature, just as the only-begotten Son, who says: *I and the Father are one* <note>John 10:30</note>. And again: *He who has seen me has seen the Father* <note>John 14:9</note>. And again: *Everything that the Father has is mine* <note>John 16:15</note>. And again: *If I do not do the works of my Father, do not believe me* <note>John 10:37</note>. And he would not have been so easily cast down. How is it that, even after being cast out, he still possessed the form of God, and his robe, and his creative power? For it would have been necessary for him first to be stripped of these things, and then to be cast down. Since this part of their story appears implausible and devoid of all truth, it follows that the subsequent claims are also seen to be false and groundless. 

One must also observe how they bring forth testimony from the book of Moses <note>The Book of Genesis, which the Bogomils generally rejected as being the work of the demiurge, yet they still cited it to support their own myths.</note>—which they otherwise reject—stating that "in the beginning God created the heaven and the earth." However, as for their dogma that Satanael created the firmament, and that he gathered the waters into the gatherings prepared for them, and that he adorned the firmament, the earth, and everything following, up to the breathing of life into the first-formed man—they stole these things from the Mosaic <term>cosmogony</term> <note>original: κοσμογονείας (kosmogoneias); the account of the origin of the universe.</note>. But the Bogomils attributed the creation of these things to Satanael, following the doctrine of the Paulicians <note>A 7th-century dualist Christian sect that influenced the later Bogomils; they believed the world was created by an evil demiurge.</note>. For those people assign to the Evil One not only the creation of these things, but also that of the first heaven and the earth, and to put it simply, of all worldly things. And look in the work *Against the Paulicians* under the chapters titled "That there are not two principles, but one creator of heaven and earth and what is in between," and you will find in those the appropriate refutations which fit the present doctrine of the Bogomils.

### XV. What they affirmed regarding the creation of things in general, they specifically intended regarding **MAN** as to his body; and they wrapped his production in much more ridiculous fables.

Namely, that Satanael, from mud,

...mixed with water, he fashioned the form of an upright man. From this body, a drop then trickled down through the big toe of the right foot; as it flowed in a circular motion, it took on the form of a serpent. This matter later received a spirit when the soul—which Satanael <note>The Bogomil name for the fallen angel/Demiurge, combining "Satan" with the divine suffix "-el."</note> had intended to share with the human body—flowed down through the body in a similar way and was poured out onto the ground like a drop, creating a living and crawling serpent. Satanael, therefore, frustrated in his hope, implored the help of God the Father. Having sent messengers to Him, he obtained through prayers the request that God would provide His own spirit to the human body Satanael had formed. For in this way, the human would belong to both of them, and could eventually occupy the seats left vacant by the fallen angels. According to these people, the Supreme Father agreed to this desire and brought it about that the human, now provided with a soul, was numbered among the living.

Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk whose work *Panoplia Dogmatica* is a primary source for Bogomil beliefs.</note> narrates the matter in this way in Section VII:

Then, having fashioned the body of Adam from earth mixed with water, he stood him upright. A certain moisture trickled down from him onto the right foot, and being poured out through the big toe onto the ground, it flowed in a coiled manner and completed the form of a serpent. Satanael, having gathered the spirit within himself, breathed life into the fashioned body; however, his spirit, due to rarity <note>The original Greek uses *chaunotēta* (χαυνότητα). The author notes that the Latin translator rendered this as "void" (*vacuum*), but he prefers "rarity" or "thinness," suggesting the Bogomils believed the spirit was too fine to stay within the physical body.</note>—which flowed down similarly to the right foot and was poured through the big toe—descended into the coiled drop. This drop was immediately brought to life, and being severed from the toe, it became a serpent and crawled away. For this reason, the serpent became wise and intelligent, as the breath of Satanael had settled into it as a soul. When this new creator saw this and realized he was laboring in vain, he sent an embassy to the Good Father <note>original: "τὸν ἀγαθὸν Πατέρα"</note> (I think the word "and" has been lost from the context here, which is required to complete the sense) and besought that a breath be sent from Him, promising that the human would be shared between them...

...[that] the human, if he were given life <note>original Greek: ζωοποιηθείς; the author notes it might be better read as ζωοποιηθείη, "if he should be given life"</note> would, through his offspring, fill the places in heaven left by the cast-down angels. But God, being good, nodded His assent and breathed a spirit of life into the figure fashioned by Satanael; and immediately the human became a living soul, which illumined the body and brightened it with many graces. From these things, it is gathered that these people held the same opinion regarding the nature and parts of man as pleased so many others who traced the origin of man to a double principle: Good and Evil. For these thinkers believed the body proceeded from Evil, but the soul from the Good, as if it were a certain more divine spirit which, having been plucked from the divine essence, thus exists as the principle of all good within the human. 

Thus established—to say nothing of others—were the Gnostics, the Manichaeans, and the Massalians <note>A 4th-century ascetic sect, also known as Euchites, who were often accused of dualism and believed a demon inhabited every person from birth.</note>, about whom I remember writing more extensively elsewhere. But if we wish to penetrate more deeply into the mind of the Bogomils, we will understand more clearly the things which Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the 12th-century Byzantine theologian who is the primary source for this description.</note> has hinted at rather than explicitly explained here, by looking at those opinions which their ancestors defended. 

For it is well known that both the Manichaeans and the Massalians, whom we have often compared with our Bogomils, attributed three parts to the human: namely, the body, a good soul from the Good Principle, and an Evil soul instilled by the Evil Principle. Regarding the Manichaeans, see Augustine's book *On Heresies*, heresy XLVI, and B. Jacob Thomasius <note>Jakob Thomasius (1622–1684), a notable German philosopher and scholar of the history of ideas.</note> in the dissertation where he explained the Manichaean dogma concerning the location of souls after death on the "ship of the moon," which can be read in Volume I of the *History of Wisdom and Folly*, page 61 and following. That the same doctrine is correctly attributed to the Massalians is clear from their chapters in Cotelerius <note>Jean-Baptiste Cotelier (1626–1686), a French scholar who edited early Christian texts.</note>, Volume I of the *Monuments*, page 302. 

Accordingly, I interpret the mind of the Bogomils to be that they believed the spirit shared with the human by Satanael did not indeed leave the human entirely, but remained in him in part, alongside the more divine spirit which—perfe- <note>The text breaks off mid-word; the catchword indicates the word would be "perfected."</note>

...had completed, it was joined together. Furthermore, it is concluded from this that they said individual humans, besides their own selves, carry about an inhabiting evil demon, as we shall mention shortly.

XVI. Let us see now how Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the 12th-century Byzantine monk and author of the *Panoplia Dogmatica*, our primary source for these beliefs.</note> was engaged in refuting these fever dreams of theirs. "But that," he says, "not even the body of the first-fashioned man <note>original Greek: τοῦ πρωτοπλάστου</note> is the creation of Satanael, you will again find wisely explained in the following chapters, which are likewise titled, 'That there is one creator of the body and the soul, that is, of man.' For we shall pass over their mythology concerning the serpent, as it is worthy of laughter rather than refutation. By what mediator did the fallen enemy make his embassy to the Father and God whom he had plotted against? For none of those who rebelled with him would have dared to undertake this. And again, none of the ministering angels with God wished to mediate for one who was an enemy to himself. How, too, would the good God have fulfilled the request of the enemy concerning the sending down of a soul to the body fashioned by him, so that man might be shared? For what fellowship has light with darkness? Or what agreement has God with Belial?" <note>Zigabenus is quoting the Apostle Paul here (2 Corinthians 6:14-15) to point out the logical impossibility of a "joint venture" between God and the Devil.</note>

XVII. In explaining the **FALL** of man, the Bogomils hold to their old ways, and, covering fictions with fictions, they leave nothing remaining that might constitute a monument of recklessness and madness. For, having abandoned the narrative of the Sacred Book, they turn themselves to allegories, and they place the reason for the fall in the intercourse of Satanael—insinuating himself into the serpent—with Eve. They imagine this was prepared by Satan with the intention that his seed might overcome the seed of Adam. Nor do they say the demon failed in his aim, since Cain—whom John testifies was of the evil one <note>A reference to 1 John 3:12, which says Cain "was of that wicked one."</note>—was then conceived from this intercourse; he who shortly after displayed an example of iniquity against his brother Abel, whom the same Eve [conceived] from Adam, who followed the example of Satan, and...

It also describes the Bogomil's alternative "Fall of Man," where Cain is depicted as the literal offspring of Satanael and Eve.</summary>

...and seized by a zeal for emulation, she conceived and gave birth. They add that the Father, moved by this very act, deprived Satanael of his divine form and his power of creation because he had taken on this stain <note>The "stain" refers to the act of seduction or the mixing of divine and corrupt natures.</note>, though he was left with authority over those things he had previously created. Let us hear Euthymius:

"Then Eve," he says in Section VII, "having been fashioned in a similar way and shining with equal brilliance, Satanael was moved to envy; he changed his mind and was moved to plot against his own handiwork. He crept into the vitals of the serpent and deceived Eve; he lay with her and made her pregnant, so that his own seed might take precedence and dominate the seed of Adam, and corrupt it as much as possible, preventing it from increasing and multiplying. She, quickly going into labor, gave birth to Cain from the union with Satanael, and a twin sister of similar character named Calomena." <note>The original Greek text uses "Καλωμενά" (Kalomena). The author notes that the Latin translator of Zigabenus renders this name as *Colonea*.</note> "Adam, being struck with jealousy, then also lay with Eve and fathered Abel. Cain immediately killed him and thereby introduced fear" <note>original Greek: *φόβον* (phobon). The author notes that the Latin translator likely read *φθόνον* (phthonon, "envy") or perhaps *φόνον* (phonon, "slaughter"), which better fits the biblical narrative.</note> "into human life. For this reason, the Apostle John said that 'Cain was of the evil one.' <note>1 John 3:12</note> However, because Satanael had been lewdly corrupted with Eve through the medium of the serpent, his divine garment and form were immediately stripped from him, as was his creative power and the title of God. For until then, he too was called 'God.' Being stripped of all these things, he became dark and hideous. But the good Father, restraining his anger until this point, permitted him to remain the ruler of this world and the Lord of those things which he himself had created after falling from above."

Therefore, it was not only in our own age that such exuberant minds were found who pursued an allegorical method of interpreting the fall of mankind, specifically regarding sexual intercourse...

<meta>catchword: vision</meta>

...they considered this union—if not with a demon, then at least between Adam and Eve—to be the origin of all evil and the first sin. To say nothing of the Manichaeans, Cathars, and Priscillians <note>Various dualist or ascetic sects throughout early and medieval church history that often held unconventional views on sexuality and the origin of the material world.</note> mentioned by Hadrian Beverland (p. 44 of *On Original Sin*), the Bogomils led the way with their own example. For this reason, Samuel Bochart thought they deserved particularly severe criticism in two letters sent to the son of Louis Cappel <note>original: "Lud. Capelli". Louis Cappel was a famous 17th-century Protestant scholar.</note> (found in Volume II of his *Works*, p. 838), as did Johannes Marckius, a theologian in Leiden in the Netherlands (Book III, ch. V). 

For anyone who wishes to read a summary of the ridiculous opinions—filling every measure of absurdity—held by various people "mad for allegories" <note>original Greek: *ἀλληγορομανῶν* (allegoromanōn), describing those who interpret every scripture as a hidden metaphor to an obsessive degree.</note>, I would recommend consulting Rivinus in his *Dissertation on the Seducing Serpent*, Thomas Burnet in his *Archaeologiae* (Book II, ch. VI, p. 494), and Pierre Bayle in Volume II of his *Dictionary* (p. 1190 ff.). 

It is worth mentioning here in passing that the Rabbis—specifically Rabbi Solomon Isaacides <note>Better known as Rashi (1040–1105), the preeminent medieval French rabbi and commentator.</note> among them—fell into a similar fabrication. However, there is this distinction: while the Rabbis say that Adam was prompted by the example of Satan to seek intimacy with Eve, the Bogomils instead trace the origin of this practice to Adam and foolishly claim that Satan turned his schemes toward deceiving the first humans—specifically Adam—so that once Adam was subjected to divine punishment, Eve would be left for him. See Rivinus (loc. cit., p. 27). Thus, those who prefer to indulge their own cleverness rather than lean upon the firmest defense of the divine Word bring back "old wives' tales" <note>original: "aniles nugas", a phrase often used to dismiss non-biblical myths as nonsense.</note> as the rewards for their folly.

XVIII. To these reported Bogomil follies, Euthymius added these critiques: "How could Satanael, being incorporeal <note>Meaning he did not have a physical, fleshly body.</note>, have had bodily intercourse with Eve? Unless, perhaps, one were to say it was through a vision. But if it was through a vision, how could he have fathered children by her?" <note>original Greek: *πῶς δ᾿ ἂν καὶ ὁ Σαταναὴλ ἀσώματος ὢν σωματικῶς τῇ Εὐᾳ συνεγένετο; εἰ μήπε κατὰ φαντασίαν εἴποι τις. Εἰ δὲ κατὰ φαντασίαν, πῶς ἂν ἐπαιδοποίησεν ἐξ αὐτῆς;*</note> Francisco Zino translates this as follows: 

*Now how did Satanael, who is without a body, lie with Eve in a bodily manner? Was it through a vision? But if through...*

...vision <note>The previous page ended with a discussion on whether the union between Eve and the devil was a physical reality or a mental "vision."</note>, how did he procreate a son from her? Since this interpretation does not align well with the Greek context, I believe it should be corrected as follows: *In truth, how could Satanael, being without a physical body, have had carnal relations with Eve, unless you claim it was achieved by means of a phantasm?* <note>original: "imaginationis beneficio". In this theological context, it refers to an illusory or "imaginary" body rather than a physical one.</note> *But if it was achieved by means of a phantasm, how did he receive children from her?* 

How then, Euthymius continues, *was Cain, having become his son, not equal and like to him, but rather to Adam? For every offspring is by nature equal and like to its begetter. But the Apostle John said that Cain was "of the evil one" [1 John 3:12], not as if he were physically born from the evil one, but as having learned wickedness from him. For a student is often called a child, and in turn, the teacher is called a father. Since it has been demonstrated that Satan is the creator of none of the worldly creatures—as was written in the chapters against the Paulicians* <note>The Paulicians were an earlier dualist sect whose rejection of the material world heavily influenced Bogomil theology.</note>, *to which we referred a little earlier—therefore, God did not appoint him as the world-ruler and lord of any of the creatures in the world.* 

*But since the word "world"* <note>original Greek: "κόσμος" (kosmos)</note> *naturally has many meanings, one of the things it signifies is worldly malice and depravity—concerning which the Lord said to the disciples, "If you were of the world, the world would love its own" [John 15:19]—he is called the <term>world-ruler</term> <note>original Greek: "κοσμοκράτωρ" (kosmokratōr). In Gnostic and Bogomil thought, this title implies the devil's dominion over the physical realm.</note> of this malice and this alone. He created this malice by fleeing from what is good, and through it, he rules and lords over those who are enticed by it and who voluntarily subject themselves to him.*

XIX. What they thought concerning the Angels can be known in part from these things, a point which we shall now examine more carefully. As far as it concerns their Prince, whom they call Satanael, they falsely claim that he was begotten from the Father along with Christ, and that he was superior to Him in rank and time; for they say that he, holding the second place after the Father in the administration of the universe, was once established in that same high dignity. They hand down that he fell from this because he aspired to a greater dignity, and rejecting the command of the Father, he meditated on de-

...thought about rebellion, and moreover swept away the ministers of the Spirit into the fellowship of his defection. They say that once this matter was known, the Father took notice of the insolence and pride of Satanael with a just punishment; having been deprived of his prerogative, he was cast headlong to the earth along with the rest of his companions in rebellion. I have as my source *Euthymius* <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and author of the "Panoplia Dogmatica," an arsenal of arguments against heresies.</note> in Section VI, who speaks as follows:

*They say that the demon named Satan by the Savior is himself also a son of God the Father, named Satanael;* <note>The name Satanael adds the suffix "-el" (meaning "of God"), implying he was a divine being before his fall, after which the Bogomils claim he lost the suffix and became merely "Satan."</note> *and that he was prior to the Son and the Word, and stronger, as being the first-born, so that these two are brothers to one another. They claim that Satanael was the steward and second-in-command to the Father, possessing the same form and clothing as Him, sitting at His right hand upon a throne, and being considered worthy of the honor immediately following Him. Drunk with this honor, and lifted up in his imagination, he contemplated apostasy; and once, having seized an opportunity, he made an attempt upon some of the liturgical powers* <note>In this context, "liturgical powers" refers to the ranks of angels whose "liturgy" or service was the worship and administration of heaven.</note> *to see if they would be willing to be lightened from the burden of their service and follow him in rising up against the Father. To lend credit to this nonsense, they bring forward the parable in the Gospel according to Luke concerning the "unjust steward" who reduced the debts of the debtors* <note>See Luke 16:1–13. The Bogomils interpreted this parable allegorically, seeing the steward as Satanael "reducing the debt" (the labor/service) of the angels to win their loyalty.</note>; *for they say this steward is Satanael, and that this parable was written about him. Then they say the aforementioned angels were lured by the lightening of their laborious service and by other boastful promises—for he said, "I will set my throne upon the clouds, and I will be like the Most High"* <note>A reference to Isaiah 14:13–14, traditionally associated with the fall of Lucifer.</note>—*to be led away with him and share in the plot. But God, sensing this, threw them all down from above together.*

By these words, the primary assertion of the *Manichaeans* <note>Manichaeism was an ancient dualist religion that taught a fundamental struggle between a good spiritual world of light and an evil material world of darkness.</note> is attributed to the *Bogomils*, whom we see have proceeded to such a point of impudence that they established a double origin for the universe, from which good and evil are derived. Indeed, if we judge the matter correctly, the *Bogomils* surpass the recklessness of those others, which they reveal while they were feigning that the very principle of evil was generated from the divine essence of the Father—a doctrine the absurdity of which the *Manichaeans* would have voluntarily...

<page-num>77</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...it never entered the minds of those who acknowledge these things. Therefore, they claimed that both principles—although by a similarly gross error—existed from eternity, as appears from the testimonies I cited in my work *Manichaeism before the Manichaeans*, page 225. Nor were they the only ones to descend into these delusions; even in our own memory, the names of **Weigel** and **Boehme** <note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588) and Jacob Boehme (1575–1624) were influential German mystics. Their ideas about the nature of God and the origin of evil were often viewed as heretical or "Manichaean" by orthodox church authorities.</note>, which are hostile to the purer Church, prove that these ideas have found supporters. See, I pray, **Ehregott Daniel Colberg’s** *Hermetic-Platonic Christianity*, Chapter III, Part II, Section 14, and **Abraham Hinckelmann** in his *Discovery of the Boehmian Foundation*. 

They hand down, therefore, that this "second God" <note>Referring to Satanael in the Bogomil hierarchy.</note> was deprived of the prerogative of his dignity, relying (as is their custom) on passages of Scripture which everyone understands to refer to something else. Consequently, it does not seem surprising to me that **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the Byzantine theologian introduced on the previous page.</note> observed these dreams of theirs with rather sharp words.

> Oh, the shamelessness, he says, of this obvious myth-making! <note>original Greek: "Ὡ τῆς ἀναιδεστάτης... μυθοπλασίας!"</note> For if Satanael were a son of God **by nature**, he would also be God **by nature**, and entirely unchangeable and immutable. For immutability and being always the same is the unique property of the Divine Nature. But since he changed and fell, and was cast down from above, he was not a son of God **by nature**, but rather some other nature—angelic and created—and for this reason, he was subject to change. 
> 
> Furthermore, if the Word of the Father is **Only-begotten** <note>term: Monogenes (μονογενής) — A Greek term meaning "one of a kind" or "only-begotten," used in the Gospel of John to describe the unique relationship between Jesus and God the Father.</note>, as **John the Evangelist** taught—for he says: *We beheld His glory, glory as of the only-begotten from the Father*—and again: *The only-begotten Son, who is in the bosom of the Father, He has declared Him*—then those who say that Satanael is His brother are talking nonsense and are out of their minds. For if He had a brother, the Word would not be named the "Only-begotten." And if the Word was in the beginning, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God, according to John the Evangelist—that is, He was always with the Father and in the Father—how could there be another brother before Him, who is pre-eternal and co-eternal with the Father? The Father was never without Him, lest He be found without Reason, without Wisdom, and without Power; for this is what the Son and Word is **by nature**. But since Satanael was not...

<meta>This page continues the theological critique of Bogomilism, focusing on the nature of the Son of God and the sect's controversial teachings regarding the power of demons. It follows a discussion on the alleged brotherhood between Christ and Satanael.</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

> By nature, the Son of God—as we have certainly demonstrated—neither wore the form or garment of God (to pass over the fact that garments and coverings belong to physical and composite bodies), nor did he sit at the right hand of the majesty of the throne; nor was the parable concerning the **unjust steward** in the Gospel according to Luke set forth regarding him. <note>referring to Luke 16:1–13; the Bogomils likely interpreted the "steward" as a figure representing their demiurge or Satanael.</note>

I observe here that the Latin translator has rendered those words of **Euthymius** somewhat inappropriately: *Prior to him who is eternal and co-without-beginning with the Father*, which he translated as: *who was older by birth, who is before the ages, and rules together with the Father.* For the word **co-without-beginning** <note>original Greek: συνάναρχος (*synanarchos*)</note> does not denote one who rules with another, but one who, along with another, is devoid of any beginning. The translator seems to have read *synenarchos* <note>original Greek: συνέναρχος</note>, a word which I believe appears nowhere and is entirely foreign to this passage, where Euthymius is attempting to show that it could not have been possible for Satanael to be "older by birth" than Christ, since Christ has always existed and acknowledges no origin.

XX. They attribute such great power to these demons and wicked spirits—power which they claim neither Christ nor the Holy Spirit can resist—that they believe it is restricted only by the boundaries set by the Father alone. Therefore, they do not fear to proclaim that these spirits must be pursued with worship, lest they bring harm to the pious. In support of this opinion, they bring forward a "divine precept," recorded in their own version of the Gospels as follows: *Worship demons, not so they may benefit you, but so they may not harm you.* Indeed, lest any fabrication be missing, they even say that not even Christ could obtain this from the Father through prayers: that he be allowed to overthrow their entire race. The words of **Euthymius** in Section XX are these: 

> The leader of their heresy <note>Likely referring to Basil the Physician, the Bogomil leader burned at the stake c. 1118.</note> said that it was written in their Gospels as a "voice of the Lord" saying: *Honor the demons, not that you may be helped by them, but that they may not harm you.* And interpreting such a saying, he added that one must honor the demons dwelling in man-made temples, by bowing down...

The author also provides a philological correction of a Latin translation concerning the eternal nature of Christ versus Satanael.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Bogomils' belief in the power of demons. It transitions from the previous page's point about worshipping spirits out of fear to a scholarly commentary comparing these beliefs to other non-Christian traditions and historical heresies.</meta>

<page-num>79</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...them, so that they might not, being provoked to anger, destroy those who do not do this; for they possess a great and invincible power for harm, against which neither Christ is sufficient, nor with Him the Holy Spirit, because the Father still spares them and has not yet taken away their strength, but has allowed them the lordship of the entire world until the consummation <note>The "consummation" refers to the end of time or the final judgment.</note>. For indeed, they say even the Son, when first sent into the world, requested their complete destruction, but did not obtain it due to the goodness of the Father.

The translator neglected to translate the words: <term>worshipping them</term> <note>original Greek: προσκυνοῦντας αὐτὰ (*proskynountas auta*)</note>, that is, **adoring them**, perhaps because he thought it was already clearly understood under the very name of "worship." You may truly marvel that the Bogomils embraced an opinion taken from the rites of non-Christian peoples, and furthermore, dared to seek support for it from the divine scriptures (as they called them)—something which, as far as I remember, not even the **Manichaeans** <note>An ancient dualistic religion that influenced many medieval sects; they believed in a struggle between a good spiritual world of light and an evil material world of darkness.</note> asserted. Regarding foreign and less-civilized nations, it is evident from various travel descriptions that this error—concerning the religious worship of both principles [good and evil] which they imagined—was widespread among them. 

Regarding the inhabitants of **Cheremiss Tartary** <note>Referring to the Mari people of the Volga region in Russia.</note>, **Olearius** in *Travels in Muscovy and Persia*, book IV, p. 179; regarding the inhabitants of the island of **Formosa** <note>modern-day Taiwan</note>, **Mandelsloh**, book III of his *Travels*, p. 162; regarding the **Beninese** of Africa, **Clericus**, Volume II of the *Universal Library*, p. 368; regarding the people of **Fetu** <note>A kingdom in the modern-day Gold Coast/Ghana.</note>, **Johann Wilhelm Müller** in his *Description of African Regions*, p. 43; regarding the **Hungarians**, **Bonfinius**, book XII, p. 223 of *Hungarian Affairs*; and regarding the **Germans**, **Hachenbergius**, dissertation VIII on *Medieval Germany*, have all left testimonies of this, to say nothing of other nations. 

For this reason, **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and theologian who wrote the "Panoply of Doctrine."</note> also briefly disposes of the refutation of this error. "Since," he says, "they learned this nonsense from the heresy of the **Messalians** <note>A 4th-century sect that believed every person was born with a demon that could only be expelled through intense, constant prayer.</note>, look for the fifth chapter in the section against them, and the eight following, and you will find the refutations in those places to be irrefutable. And these things have also been mentioned above in the twelfth chapter of the present—"

<page-num>80</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

<meta>catchword: absurdity</meta>

...of the title. Since they even say that demons are stronger than both Christ and the Holy Spirit, what punishment would these god-fighters <note>original Greek: θεομάχοι (*theomachoi*)</note> and blasphemers not be worthy of—they who are worshipers and servants of demons, being themselves demonic and lovers of demons?

**XXI.** They undoubtedly believed that an argument for this power of demons over men should be sought from the fact that they did not doubt that individual demons inhabit individual men. They claimed these demons are the authors of crimes and impurity within people, and do not depart from their bodies, but eventually undergo the punishments destined for those committed crimes along with the people themselves. **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and author of the "Panoply of Doctrine," a major source on medieval heresies.</note> in Section XIII writes as follows:

> They say that demons always flee from them alone—that is, from the **Bogomils**—as if from the flight of an arrow, but that a demon dwells in every one of all other people, teaching them evil things, leading them to unholy deeds, and when a person dies, the demon dwells again in their remains and stays in the grave to await the resurrection, so that it may be punished with the person, never being separated from them even in punishment.

Here stands a new trace of pagan error, which first the **Messalians** <note>Also known as Euchites, this 4th-century sect believed everyone was born with an indwelling demon that could only be expelled through constant prayer.</note>, and after them our Bogomils, adopted. For many of the **Gentiles** <note>Refers to non-Christian Greeks and Romans.</note> also assigned two **Genii** <note>The "Genius" was a protective or guiding spirit in Roman religion; here the author compares them to the Bogomil demons.</note> or spirits to every individual—one good and the other evil. They said that the former stirred the human soul toward the pursuit of virtue, while the latter moved it toward every kind of crime. See **Servius** <note>A 4th-century commentator on the works of Virgil.</note> on Virgil’s *Aeneid*, Book V, line 85, and others whose testimonies were brought forward by **Steuchus Eugubinus** in *On Perennial Philosophy* VIII. 23. 31. and 40; **Huetius** in the *Alnetan Questions, or on the Concord of Reason and Faith* II. 4. p. 134 and following; and **Dionysius Petavius** in Volume III of *Theological Dogmas*, chapter 7, p. 72 and following. Euthymius added this brief observation:

> This idea that a demon dwells in every man is taken from the heresy of the **Messalians**; look for the fifth chapter and the eight following ones in the title written against them, and you will learn the... <note>The text breaks off mid-sentence as it moves to the next page.</note>

<summary>This page discusses the Bogomil belief that every non-Bogomil is possessed by an indwelling demon that drives them to sin and remains with them even after death. The author identifies the roots of this belief in the earlier Messalian heresy and ancient Roman concepts of good and evil spirits (Genii).</summary>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.
M

<meta>catchword: undertaken,</meta>

...absurdity of the doctrine <note>This continues the sentence from page 80, which argued that the Bogomil belief in indwelling demons was illogical.</note>. Once the premise is proven false, the conclusion is also shown to be false—I mean the part regarding the remains [of the dead] and the indwelling of demons within them. Furthermore, if a demon remained with every person who died to await the final resurrection, the supply of demons would have been exhausted. For from the time of Adam until now, countless millions of people have died. That demons do not flee from the **Bogomils** <note>A medieval dualist sect that believed the material world was created by an evil or fallen figure.</note> alone, but rather live alongside them and speak openly to them, is clear from their other delusions and from the fact that demons appear to them in various forms, as you will find described further down.

The Latin translator omits these last words, in which **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the 12th-century Byzantine theologian being cited here.</note> refers back to the things he will say a little later regarding the appearance of demons to the Bogomils. The same translator seems to have read "myriads of men" <note>original Greek: ἀνθρώπων μυριάδες (*anthrōpōn myriades*)</note> instead of "countless myriads of people," and not without reason, as both the grammar and the sense of the passage require it.

**XXII.** They [the Bogomils] said that the fallen demons were anxious for the restoration of their former status. They say the demons devised a plan to reclaim their former seats in heaven for their own kind by procreating children with women. They thought this would happen because **Satanael** <note>In Bogomil theology, Satanael was the elder son of God and the creator of the physical world before his fall.</note> had promised the Father that humans would occupy the dwellings the demons had vacated. In this way, they believe they can interpret the passage in **Genesis 6:2** <note>The biblical verse: "the sons of God saw that the daughters of humans were beautiful, and they married any of them they chose."</note>. I have Euthymius as my source:

> They say that the fallen angels heard that Satanael had promised the Father that the places in heaven would be filled by the human race. [Because of this], they looked lustfully upon the daughters of men and took them as wives, so that their own offspring might ascend into heaven, back to the places of their fathers. For they say that when the "sons of God" saw that the daughters of men were beautiful, they took wives for themselves; and they call these beings "sons of God," as if they were descended from that original source <note>meaning Satanael</note>.

From this union, they say the **Giants** <note>The Nephilim of the Bible, interpreted here as the hybrid offspring of fallen angels.</note> were born, who, in a war with Satanael...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Bogomil mythology regarding the Great Flood and the "Sons of God." It connects the Bogomil belief in Satanael's role in the flood to various historical theological interpretations of Genesis 6.</meta>

<page-num>82</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...having undertaken [it], they fought on behalf of men and came out superior. Satanael <note>In Bogomil doctrine, Satanael is the elder son of God and the creator of the material world who rebelled against the Father.</note>, resenting this and fearing for his own interests, ordered a flood of waters to occupy the whole world. In this way, he intended to take the lives of the men who were hostile to him, with the exception only of Noah, whom he still held as obedient to his laws.

"From their intercourse," <note>original Greek: Ἐκ δὲ τῆς συνεσίας αὐτῶν (*Ek de tēs synesias autōn*)</note> Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and author of the *Panoplia Dogmatica*.</note> thus continues, "the giants were born. These giants stood against Satanael and triumphed over his apostasy <note>Meaning they revealed his rebellion against the true God to humanity.</note> on behalf of mankind. But Satanael, being enraged, brought the flood upon them and destroyed all living flesh along with them. Only Noah, who did not possess a daughter, remained ignorant of Satanael's apostasy and continued to serve him. It was Satanael, pleased by this worship, who suggested the plans for the ark and saved Noah alone along with those inside it."

Indeed, as far as this explanation of that passage is concerned, I would not deny that it has appealed to several ancient as well as more recent interpreters. Some of them, such as **Lactantius** <note>A 4th-century advisor to Constantine who wrote about the corruption of angels.</note>, thought the passage should be understood as referring to good angels; others, like **Lyra** and **Estius** <note>Nicholas of Lyra (d. 1340) and Willem Estius (d. 1613), prominent biblical commentators.</note>, thought it referred to bad angels. On this matter, one may read what **Natalis Alexander** <note>A 17th-century Dominican church historian.</note> recorded in his seventh dissertation on the First Age of the Old Testament. 

But just as those authors found very few supporters for that opinion—most people following the interpretation which Euthymius urges in the following words—so no one could think of Giants born from that union, or waters sent specifically to destroy them, except for those whose minds were thoroughly nurtured on ridiculous fictions. This is understood more fully from the following words of Euthymius:

"These are their [the Bogomils'] nonsensical ravings" <note>original Greek: Ταῦτα μὲν ἐκείνων τὰ ληρήματα (*Tauta men ekeinōn ta lērēmata*)</note>. "We, however, say concerning the shameful intercourse and procreation of these fallen angels exactly what we said above regarding Satanael's lustful intercourse and fathering of children with Eve. Thus, the Scripture called the descendants of Seth 'Sons of God' <note>original Greek: Υἱὸς μὲν οὖν τοῦ Θεοῦ (*Huios men oun tou Theou*)</note>, for Seth was named a god because of his God-imitating life and the blamelessness of his conduct; but the 'daughters of...'"

<meta>This page continues the refutation of Bogomil teachings regarding the Great Flood and transitions into their controversial views on "Regeneration" and the status of the Virgin Mary.</meta>

<page-num>83</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...of men, specifically those from the race of Cain and those descended from him. <note>original Greek: ἀνθρώπων, τὰς ἀπὸ τοῦ γένους τοῦ Καϊν (*anthrōpōn, tas apo tou genous tou Kaïn*)</note> Therefore, it was not Satanael <note>The Bogomil demiurge</note> who brought the flood, but God upon those who were destroyed; the very God whom Noah knew and worshiped, and by whom he was entrusted with the construction of the ark. This was the Lawgiver of the Old Testament. That this is truly and properly God has been clearly demonstrated before in the section against the **Paulicians** <note>A dualist Christian sect that preceded and influenced the Bogomils</note>, specifically in the chapters titled: *That both the Old Lawgiving and those who shone within it belong to God*, as well as in the chapters following those.

XXIII. What they thought concerning **REGENERATION** can be understood from the accounts provided by **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century monk</note> in Section XXII. For they were convinced that through this regeneration, they attained such a state that they could be called "Parents of God" <note>original Latin: *Dei Parentes*</note> by the same right that Mary earned that title. They claimed to conceive the Word of God, carry it in their womb, and finally bring it forth at the time when they are occupied in instructing others and leading them to the truth; and thus, they believed there was no special privilege for which Mary could boast. Euthymius explains the matter thus:

> They say that as many of their faith as are inhabited by the Holy Spirit are both called and truly are **Theotokoi** <note>original Greek: Θεοτόκους (*Theotokous*) — literally "God-bearers" or "Mothers of God." This is the traditional title for the Virgin Mary in Orthodox theology.</note>, having themselves also carried the Word of God and given birth to Him through the teaching of others; they claim that the first Mother of God has nothing more than they do.

The scholar **Harmenopulus** <note>Constantine Harmenopulus, a 14th-century Byzantine jurist and theologian</note> records the same:

> They call those who have acquired the Holy Spirit dwelling within them "Theotokoi," as they give birth to the Word through their teaching.

Indeed, they even dare to strip away from Mary that which the Holy Scriptures grant her as a unique proof of divine grace. They do not fear to deny the truth of the divine **Incarnation** <term>enanthropēseōs: the theological concept of God becoming human in Jesus</term> so that they might place themselves upon a higher pinnacle of dignity. And in this matter—their lowly opinion of Mary—they once again imitate the **Manichaeans** <note>Followers of Mani; a major dualist religion that the Church considered the root of later heresies</note>. The author **Photius** <note>9th-century Patriarch of Constantinople</note> notes in his first book against the Manichaeans (in manuscript) that they did not restrain themselves from casting insults upon the Most Holy Mother.

<page-num>84</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.
<meta>catchword: struck</meta>

...and blaspheming our most holy Lady, the Mother of God <note>original Greek: *Theotokos*</note>, in ways that are lawful to accept neither in writing nor by hearing. These men—who deserve to perish not once, but three times over—are naturally inclined to say: "We believe in the all-holy Mother of God, into whom the Lord entered and from whom He exited." <note>The Bogomils viewed Mary merely as a physical vessel or "conduit" through which Christ passed, rather than a true mother, often comparing her to a bag or a pipe.</note> **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, the primary Byzantine source for Bogomil beliefs</note> briefly refutes this Bogomil opinion in the aforementioned passage: 

-> "And they do not even possess enough sense to know the difference between the living Word that exists as a person <note>original Greek: *enypostatou* — a technical theological term meaning "having a real, personal existence"</note> and a simply <note>The editor adds: perhaps it should read "concerning the Word," or at least "and" should be added before the word "simply"</note> spoken word, so that we might pass over the terrifying and paradoxical mysteries concerning the true Mother of God." <-

XXIV. Thus, although they spoke so magnificently of their own **Regeneration**, they judged its means—which the more divine Scripture prescribed and intended to be used—quite sinfully and despicably. They were not unlike those in our own times who wish to be bound by the means of salvation and yet do not doubt they can achieve what the Divine Power does not grant without them. They held both **SACRAMENTS** in contempt. They looked down upon the Baptism received in the holy assembly as if it were performed only through water, preferring their own, which they claimed was conferred through the Spirit. They attributed the former to the authorship of John [the Baptist] and the latter to Christ. In this, they clearly preceded the words of those today who distinguish between external and internal Baptism in such a way that they impiously declare the former can be absent from the Church. Euthymius discusses it thus: 

> They say that the baptism among us is that of John, as it is performed through water, but that the baptism among them is that of Christ, being performed (as it seems to them) through the Spirit; for this reason, they rebaptize those who join them. 

So too **Harmenopulus** <note>Constantine Harmenopulus, a 14th-century judge and compiler of church law</note> records: *saying the baptism among us is that of John, but that among them is that of the Savior.* For this reason, in the *Refutation of the Massalians* <note>The Massalians or Messalians were an older heretical sect whose ideas on spiritual prayer influenced the Bogomils</note>, which is found in the work of **Tollius** <note>Jacobus Tollius (1633–1696), a Dutch classicist and traveler</note> titled *Notable Records of an Italian Journey*, page 120, this very belief—by which Sacred Baptism is regarded as mere water—is struck by a thunderbolt <note>original Latin: *fulmine feriri* — a metaphor for being condemned or excommunicated</note>

<summary>This page describes the Bogomils' rejection of the orthodox status of the Virgin Mary and their dismissal of water baptism as merely "the baptism of John," replacing it with their own spiritual initiation.</summary>
<keywords>Bogomils, Theotokos, Virgin Mary, Baptism, Euthymius Zigabenus, Harmenopulus, Sacraments, Massalians, Jacobus Tollius</keywords>

<page-num>85</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...you may see struck. In this same way, the Manichaeans <note>An ancient dualist religion that influenced later medieval sects like the Bogomils</note> once used to pretend that they accepted baptism, though in reality they judged that it contributed nothing to the business of salvation. Photius <note>A 9th-century Patriarch of Constantinople and influential scholar</note> testifies to this above others in his first book *Against the Manichaeans* (Manuscript):

-> "Furthermore, while they treat the saving baptism with contempt <note>original Greek: *diaptyontes* — literally 'spitting upon'</note>, they feign its acceptance by substituting the words of the Gospel for the actual ritual of baptism. For they say: 'The Lord said, I am the living water.'" <-

Join to this **Augustine** in chapter 46 of his book *On Heresies*, and especially the *Formula for the Reception of Manichaeans* found in Tollius on page 147 of the *Insignia Itinerarii Italici*, where those words are read which for the most part match the account of Photius. I omit the others who, out of antiquity, attempted by every means to diminish and belittle the power of baptism as received by the Church; nor do I mention those more recent figures who have occasionally played the same tune <note>original Latin: *tibias subinde inflarunt* — an idiom for repeating the same old ideas or errors</note>, having been deservedly corrected by our own **Magnificent Wernsdorf** <note>Gottlieb Wernsdorf (1664–1729), a Lutheran theologian who wrote on the history of doctrines</note> in his dissertation *On recent controversies pertaining to Baptism.*

Let us now hear how **Euthymius** <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and the primary source for the trial of the Bogomil leader Basil the Physician</note>, in the passage already praised, refutes the opinion of the Bogomils:

-> "If, then," he says, "our baptism were performed with water alone, it would be the baptism of John. But since it is perfected not by water alone, but also by the **Holy Spirit**, as the Lord taught when He said: *Unless one is born of water, he cannot enter the kingdom of God*, they bark in vain <note>original Greek: *matēn hylaktousin*</note>. Moreover, more accurate things have been said regarding the holy baptism in the chapter against the Paulicians <note>An earlier sect from whom the Bogomils derived many of their dualist views</note>; look there for the main points on this subject. But let them show from where their own baptism was handed down; in which of the seven books accepted by them is it written concerning this? It remains, therefore, that they learned this from demons, so that they might be stripped of the true and divine baptism." <-

Indeed, this is the very same thing that was stated long ago concerning the water joined with the Spirit in the divine washing by the divine Doctors and Leaders <note>original Latin: *Antistites* — high-ranking clergy or bishops</note> of the early Church...

<page-num>86</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...forefathers <note>The text begins mid-word with "-stites," likely "antistites," referring to the leaders or bishops of the early Church</note> warned. Cyril of Jerusalem <note>A 4th-century bishop and theologian</note> specifically in his third *Catechesis*, section 2, instructs the unlearned in this way: "Do not approach the washing as if it were simple water, but rather with the spiritual grace given along with the water." <note>original Greek: μὴ ὡς ὕδατι λιτῷ πρόσελθε τῷ λοερῷ, ἀλλὰ τῇ μετὰ τοῦ ὕδατος διδομένῃ πνευματικῇ χάριτι</note> John Chrysostom <note>An influential Early Church Father known for his preaching</note> speaks to the same effect in his 35th homily on the fifth chapter of John, as does Paulinus <note>Likely Paulinus of Nola</note> in his 12th letter to Serenus.

XXV. They held so stubbornly to the opinion they had absorbed regarding Baptism that they decreed that those who had been initiated into the sacred rites by the solemn formula of baptism and the customary manner, yet wished to join their faction, must be baptized again according to their own custom. In this process, they ordered water to be kept far away. After a certain time was defined for confession and purification, they would, for the first time, place the Gospel of John upon the head of the person being admitted to the rites; then, having invoked their own "Holy Spirit," they would chant the Lord’s Prayer. However, so that no outward appearance <note>original Latin: *speciei* — meaning the formal look or "show" of a religious rite</note> should be lacking, they prescribed another interval of time during which the candidate allowed himself to be further instructed and freed more and more from the filth of a defiled life. Then, at last, they cleared the way for a second initiation, which they considered "perfection." For they commanded the person to stand facing the rising sun, and with the Gospel of John placed upon the head, both men and women performed the rite by laying on their hands, while songs were chanted in a loud voice in the meantime. It is better to hear from Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk who wrote extensively against heresies</note> himself: 

-> "For this reason," he says, "they rebaptize the one who comes to them, first setting aside a time for him for confession, purification, and intense prayer. Then, placing the Gospel according to John upon his head, and invoking the 'holy Spirit' as they understand it, they chant the *Our Father*. After such a baptism, they again allot a time for a stricter way of life, more self-controlled conduct, and purer prayer. Then they demand testimony as to whether he has kept everything and whether he has struggled zealously. And when both men and women testify..." <- <note>original Greek: Διὸ καὶ τὸν προσερχόμενον... Καὶ μαρτυρόντων ἀνδρῶν ὁμοῦ καὶ γυναικῶν</note>

<page-num>87</page-num>

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

...of women <note>The text continues from the previous page's description of the candidate's preparation</note>, they lead him to the celebrated perfection <note>original Greek: τελείωσιν (*teleiosin*), a term used in mystery religions for the final stage of initiation</note>. Having stood the wretched man toward the east, they again place the Gospel upon his defiled head; then the men and women present, laying on their polluted hands, chant the unholy rite. This is a hymn of thanksgiving, because he has preserved the handed-down impiety. And thus they initiate and "perfect"—or rather, they finish off and drown in the abyss one who is worthy of destruction. Anyone who compares these words with the Latin version will understand that some things have been omitted which contribute to the clarity of the meaning. As far as the matter itself is concerned, I seem to see in part both the method and the different levels of initiation, which the records of secular writers testify were accepted in the sacred rites of the Gentiles <note>The author is comparing Bogomil rituals to ancient pagan "mysteries"</note>. For they too, in their *Teletae* <term>Teletae: Secret initiation ceremonies or "perfections" in ancient Greek religion</term>, began with a purification, which they called a *katharmon* <note>original Greek: καθαρμὸν</note>. They were not accustomed to complete the business in one go; rather, they ordered the one aspiring to that goal to strive through various levels toward the summit of impiety. Theon of Smyrna explains this chapter of antiquity carefully in his *Mathematics*, page 19, upon whose words Ismaël Bullialdus sheds light in his exquisite notes on page 215. Compare Johannes Scheffer's book on *Italian Philosophy*, chapter 9, page 73 and following, and the author of the *Philosophumena* <note>The "Refutation of All Heresies," then attributed to Origen, now usually attributed to Hippolytus of Rome</note>—which is circulated under Origen's name—page 8. Why the initiate should have had to turn toward the Rising Sun is not obscure to anyone who remembers that the Manichaeans—whom the Bogomils diligently imitated—established that the souls of men are purified by the Sun and the Moon and are finally carried back to God. See B. Scherzer’s program on the *Transfusion of Souls into the Moon*, and Tollius in his frequently praised *Insignia*, pages 128 and 138, and also in his *Fortuita*, chapter 23. Among the ancient writers, see Epiphanius on the heresy of the Manichaeans, number 6.

# ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.

It is called by the name of the HOLY SUPPER <note>The author is discussing the Bogomil view of the Eucharist or Holy Communion</note>. For they, with an impious mouth, called that sacrifice [the Orthodox Mass] one belonging to the demons dwelling in temples, and in its place they substituted the Lord’s Prayer; indeed, they thought the matter was performed not by the mouth of the body, but only by the spirit and by faith. Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk and theologian who wrote extensively against heretical groups</note> comments thus:

<note>original Greek: Ἀτιμάζουσι τὴν μυστικὴν καὶ φρικτὴν ἱερουργίαν...</note>
They dishonor the mystical and terrifying sacred rite, and the holy communion of the Master’s body and blood, calling it a sacrifice of the demons dwelling in the temples. Oh, defiled lips! Oh, tongue more defiled!

Greater was their impudence, while they dared to give credit to this assertion with the words of Isaiah, whom they think referred to the Sacred Supper in chapter 65:11. For Euthymius continues:

<note>original Greek: Καὶ παριστῶσι μάρτυρα τὸν Προφήτην Ἠσαΐαν...</note>
And they present the Prophet Isaiah as a witness, saying: *those who prepare a table for Fortune, and fill a mixture for the demon.* <note>Isaiah 65:11. The Bogomils interpreted this biblical condemnation of idolatry as a prophecy against the Christian Eucharist</note>

How ineptly this is done, the same author makes clear in the following words:

<note>original Greek: μὴ συνιέντες οἱ ἐμπλῆγες, ὅτι πρὸς εἰδωλολατρεῦντας ὁ λόος...</note>
the foolish ones not understanding that the word is directed toward those practicing idolatry. But Christ handed down the mystical supper to us, saying: *Do this in remembrance of me,* as the Evangelists teach. It has also been discussed in the discourse against the Paulicians <note>The Paulicians were an earlier dualist sect often linked by medieval writers to the Bogomils</note> concerning this divine communion; look there for more about it.

I have as a witness of the same error **Anna Comnena** <note>Byzantine princess and historian, daughter of Emperor Alexios I Komnenos</note>, book XV of the *Alexiad*, p. 488.

<note>original Greek: Καὶ τὸ τελούμενον παρ' ἡμῖν σῶμα καὶ αἷμα...</note>
And the body and blood performed among us by the first high priest and victim [Christ], they regarded as a trifling thing.

**Harmenopulus** <note>Constantine Harmenopulus, a 14th-century Byzantine jurist</note> repeats not only the sentiment, but also most of the words of Euthymius in this manner:

<note>original Greek: Καὶ τὴν φρικτὴν ἱερουργίαν τοῦ δεσποτικοῦ σώματος...</note>
And the terrifying sacred rite of the Master’s body and blood, calling this a sacrifice of the demons dwelling in the temples.

In this they again follow in the footsteps of the Manichaeans <note>Followers of Mani; a major dualist religion that influenced later medieval heresies</note>, about whom **Photius** <note>9th-century Patriarch of Constantinople</note> records these things in manuscript book I:

<note>original Greek: ὡσαύτως καὶ τὴν κοινωνίαν τοῦ τιμίου σώματος...</note>
Likewise, having washed the communion of the precious body and blood of Christ our Lord with ten thousand insults, they say they receive the body and blood, telling tall tales about the Master’s words, which they say he gave to the ap-

...to say: *Take, eat, and drink.* <note>original Greek: ςολοις ειπεῖν. λάβετε, Φάγετε, καὶ πίετε.</note> See also the *Victoria* regarding the Messalians <note>A 4th-century sect that believed prayer alone, not sacraments, could expel demons</note>, anathema 12, and the formula for the reception of the Manichaeans on page 145 in the works of Tollius <note>Jacobus Tollius, a 17th-century Dutch scholar and editor of ancient texts</note>.

XXVII. However, they did not wish to seem entirely deprived of this divine banquet. Instead, so that they might appear to perform everything in spirit, they claimed that the place of the Eucharist was held for them by those words of the Lord’s Prayer in which daily bread is sought from God. Euthymius reports this in the same passage cited above:

<note>original Greek: ἴδιον δὲ αὐτῶν καὶ τόδε. ἄρτον μὲν γὰρ τῆς κοινωνίας ὀνομάζουσι τὴν προσευχὴν τὸ Πάτερ ἡμῶν...</note>
And this also is characteristic of them: for they call the prayer, the "Our Father," the bread of communion. For he says it is the "daily bread" <note><term>epiousios</term> — a rare Greek word in the Lord's Prayer often translated as "daily" or "super-substantial," referring to spiritual nourishment</note>. Similarly, they call the "cup of communion" that which is named the "covenant" in the Gospel. For he says, "this cup is the new covenant." And they consider the "mystical supper" to be the participation in both of these. But if anyone should counter-argue, asking how the "Our Father" is "broken" and "distributed" like the Master's body, or how to explain the verse "now is the Son of Man glorified" and what follows, or how the blood of the Savior was poured out for us—they admit they do not know.

One might perhaps trace the origin of this opinion back to the interpretation of the fourth petition [of the Lord's Prayer], which was customarily done according to the Savior’s prescription. Although that interpretation is less consistent, it was approved by the authority of more than one Doctor of the early Church, and it seems to have provided the occasion for that fantastical "eating" which they practiced. Indeed, it is a traditional and widespread view that certain Doctors of the Church—specifically Tertullian, Ambrose, Athanasius, Cyril, Augustine, and others—thought that the "daily bread" <note>original Greek: τὸν ἄρτον ἐπιούσιον</note> referred to the Eucharistic bread. It is not necessary to gather their testimonies here, as I remember Casaubon <note>Isaac Casaubon, a renowned Renaissance classical scholar</note> did so in his *Exercise XVI*, Section XXXIX, page 467, written against the *Annals* of Baronius; as did Johann Caspar Suicer in his *Ecclesiastical Thesaurus* under the entry *epiousion*; and Claude Saumaise <note>also known as Salmasius, a 17th-century French scholar</note>, who holds the same opinion in his book *On the Interest of Bankers*. However, just as this interpretation is far-fetched and lacks solid foundation, as is seen from...

<meta>This page continues the academic refutation of Bogomil theology, moving from their spiritualized interpretation of the Lord's Prayer and the Eucharist to their specific views on Justification and their rejection of traditional Church authority.</meta>

<page-num>90</page-num>

-># ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.<-

As Casaubon <note>Isaac Casaubon (1559–1614) was a prominent classical scholar and philologist who analyzed early Christian texts.</note> rightly judged in the cited passage, and as Christian Kortholt has solidly proved in our own time in his *Anti-Baronian Disquisition* regarding "daily bread," <note>original Latin: *de pane ἐπιουσίῳ*. Kortholt was a Lutheran theologian writing against the Catholic historian Cesare Baronio.</note> and more recently the celebrated Wernsdorf in his *Vindication of the Lord’s Prayer*; it is thus not obscure that the Bogomils find no defense for their opinion in this explanation. For even those Church Fathers who interpreted the bread spiritually did not wish for the actual eating of the Holy Supper to be abolished in this way, but rather recommended its daily use at the beginning of the Church.

XXVIII. No one would believe that those who so impudently attacked the most excellent heads of heavenly doctrine with an impious hand would have kept away from the very center of Christian doctrine, since that center has never ceased to be assaulted by men of this kind. Those who have ever grasped the dignity and excellence of this subject in their minds will easily understand that I am speaking of **JUSTIFICATION**. Regarding this, the Bogomils held such a position that, like the "False Apostles" of old, they believed it should be sought not from the Gospel, but from the Law—and indeed, even from human institutions. Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine monk whose *Panoplia Dogmatica* is the primary source for Bogomil beliefs.</note> does not mention Justification in express words, nor does he set out the Bogomils' specific opinion on it, but he nevertheless left an ample opportunity to reach it by a conjecture that is neither vain nor uncertain. For in Section 37, he recounts the status the Bogomils gave to their own righteousness versus the righteousness of others who departed from their approved opinions, and how insolently they called the latter "Pharisaic" and the former "genuine" and "responding to the Savior’s will." For they were accustomed to interpret Matthew 5:20 in this way:

<note>original Greek: "γραμματεῖς μὲν καὶ Φαρισαίους ἡμᾶς προσαγορεύουσιν ὡς τὴν γραμματικὴν παίδευσιν ἀσκοῦντας, καὶ μεγαλαυχοῦντας ἐπ’ αὐτῇ, περισσεύειν δὲ ἡμῶν τὴν δικαιοσύνην αὐτῶν λέγουσιν ἐν τῷ διδάσκειν ἀληθέστερα, καὶ μετιέναι πολιτείαν ἀκριβεστέραν τε καὶ καθαρωτέραν, ἀπεχομένους κρεωφαγίας, καὶ τυροῦ καὶ..."</note>

"They address us as Scribes and Pharisees because we practice grammatical education <note>The Bogomils rejected the formal academic and linguistic study of the Bible favored by the Orthodox Church.</note> and boast in it; but they say that their own righteousness exceeds ours because they teach more truthfully and pursue a more exact and purer way of life, abstaining from the eating of meat, and cheese, and..."

<page-num>91</page-num>

-># ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.<-

<sig>N 2</sig>

...of these things, including marriage and similar matters. Thus they boasted of a holier life and more rigorous institutions, and by this means they seemed to have secured for themselves a significant advantage in the divine court. Indeed, this opinion was so deeply rooted in their minds that it never ceased to sprout and reveal itself in their words, as will be made even more manifest in what follows.

### XXIX. What they thought regarding the LAW and the GOSPEL is evident even from those things mentioned above concerning Holy Scripture.

They accepted only the Gospel, having commanded the Law—whose origin they attributed to an evil Demon <note>The Bogomils were dualists who believed the material world and the Old Testament were the creation of Satanael, the elder, rebellious son of God.</note>—to be cast far away. However, they did not refrain from defiling the Gospel itself with various commentaries and twisting it into a foreign meaning. From this arose hateful and most unfair comparisons, which aimed solely at belittling the authority of the Law. With this intention, they compared the divine Law to camel's hair on account of its impurity, but the Gospel to a leather belt; furthermore, they compared the Law’s precepts to locusts, but the Gospel to wild honey. This is understood from the <term>Euthymius Zigabenus</term> <note>Euthymius Zigabenus was a 12th-century monk commissioned by the Byzantine Emperor to catalog and refute various heresies.</note> Section XXX, which reads as follows:

> "And John himself," they say, "had his clothing made from camel’s hair, and a leather belt around his waist; and his food was locusts and wild honey." These are the literal words of the Gospel. But one must also consider what those thunderstruck, mad, and god-smitten people <note>Euthymius uses these insults to describe the Bogomils' radical departure from Orthodox tradition.</note> say about these things. They say that the camel’s hair represents the commandments of the Mosaic Law. For they claim this Law is unclean like a camel, because it commands its subjects to eat meat, and permits marriage, oaths, sacrifices, killings, and many such things. But they say the leather belt is the Holy Gospel, because it is written upon sheepskins <note>original: "δέρμασι προβάτων." The Bogomils contrasted the 'unclean' camel hair with 'clean' sheepskin parchment used for holy books.</note>. And again, they say the locusts are the commands of the Mosaic Law, as things that do not stand upright or distinguish what is better. But the wild honey is the Divine Gospel; it is indeed honey to those who receive it, for it is sweet...

<summary>This page describes how the Bogomils rejected the Old Testament Law as the work of an evil demon, using allegorical interpretations of John the Baptist's clothing and diet to belittle the Mosaic Law while exalting the Gospel.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the examination of Bogomil interpretations of Scripture, specifically focusing on their rejection of the Old Testament Law (Mosaic Law) and their radical reinterpretation of the Gospel of Matthew to suit their dualist theology.</meta>

<page-num>92</page-num>

-># ON THE DOCTRINES OF THE BOGOMILS.<-

<meta>catchword: sound</meta>

He says, "Your words are sweet to my throat." <note>original: "Φησι, τῇ λάρυγγί μου τὰ λόγιά σου" — a reference to Psalm 119:103.</note> But it is "wild" to those who do not accept it because of the bitterness of the narrow gate and the hard way. For the Forerunner <note>John the Baptist</note> was the middle point between both the Old Law and the New, and shared in both: the former first, and the latter afterwards. **They spoke no more mildly about the Law when they declared it was utterly abolished by the Savior and exchanged for the Gospel in the times of the New Covenant. For so says Euthymius <note>Euthymius Zigabenus, a 12th-century Byzantine theologian.</note> in Section XLI:** "They say," he reports, "that 'you have heard it said: An eye <note>The editor notes a preference for the Greek accusative "τὸν ὀφθαλμὸν" over the nominative "ὁ ὀφθαλμὸς" found in the transcription.</note> for an eye, and a tooth for a tooth.' They claim the 'two eyes' are the two laws—the Mosaic and the Evangelical. The 'teeth' are the two paths—the broad and the narrow. For Christ, having come, gave law instead of law (the Evangelical instead of the Mosaic) and path instead of path (the narrow instead of the broad). Oh, the vast ignorance of the unlearned!" **And since they thought that the passage in Matthew 5:18—where it says not even the smallest stroke <term>apex</term> <note>Literally a "horn" or "tittle," referring to the smallest mark in Hebrew or Greek script.</note> of the law shall perish—stood in the way of this opinion, they wanted it to be understood as referring to the Jews, who observe Mosaic institutions to this very day. For so says Euthymius in Section XXXVI:** ...understanding "iota" <note>The smallest Greek letter, used here to symbolize the Ten Commandments, perhaps because the number 10 is represented by Iota.</note> as the Decalogue of the law, and the "stroke" <note>original: "κεραίαν" (keraian)</note> as the same thing again. For if anyone straightens the "stroke" which lies sideways, he will make an "iota." Then they interpret that unless the Decalogue of the law passes away, it will remain observed among the Hebrews until heaven and earth pass away. For He says, "I did not come to destroy the law of Moses, but to fill heaven" <note>The Bogomils here perform a wordplay on the Greek "plerōsai" (to fulfill). While in standard Christianity it means Jesus fulfilled the Law's requirements, the Bogomils claimed He came to "fill up" the physical space in heaven left empty by the fallen angels.</note>—which was emptied long ago of its resident angels—"and to replenish the ranks of the fallen powers." **But just as this latter explanation clearly distorts the Savior's words by stuffing in the word "heaven," <note>original: "οὐρανὸς"</note> which the Savior did not have in mind; so too the former cannot hold its ground, as it denies that the "stroke" must be distinguished from the "iota."**

### XXX. In the Article on RENOVATION, they did not follow more carefully or diligently the "pattern of sound words" <note>original: "τύπον τῶν ὑγιαινόντων λόγων" — a phrase from 2 Timothy 1:13.</note> or a *delineation*...

<summary>This page details how the Bogomils reinterpreted Biblical metaphors and specific verses (like Matthew 5:18) to argue that the Mosaic Law was entirely replaced by the Gospel and that Christ's mission was to physically refill heaven with the souls of the saved.</summary>
